## 1. Second Law Of Thermodynamics In Closed Quantum Many-Body Systems

- **Score**: 8.8 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: PRX Quantum  |  **Feed**: PRX Quantum
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-11-03
- **Authors**: Yuuya Chiba, Yasushi Yoneta, Ryusuke Hamazaki, And Akira Shimizu
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/3y8f-yrp8](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/3y8f-yrp8)
- **Tags**: `quantum thermodynamics`  `second law`  `closed quantum systems`  `many-body systems`  `emergence of irreversibility`

### TL;DR

A new theoretical framework shows how the second law of thermodynamics emerges in closed quantum many-body systems described by pure states.

### Motivation

There is a long-standing tension between the irreversibility of macroscopic thermodynamics and the unitary, reversible evolution of closed quantum systems.

### Method

The authors develop a theoretical framework analyzing thermodynamic behavior in closed quantum many-body systems, reconciling macroscopic irreversibility with quantum mechanical pure-state evolution.

### Result

They demonstrate that macroscopic thermodynamic principles, including the second law, can emerge from pure quantum states without invoking external baths or ensemble averaging.

### Conclusion

The work resolves a conceptual paradox and provides a foundation for understanding thermodynamics in isolated quantum systems.

*Keywords: quantum thermodynamics, second law, closed quantum systems, many-body systems, emergence of irreversibility*

## 3. Reliable Entropy Estimation From Observed Statistics For Device-Independent Quantum Cryptography

- **Score**: 8.7 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-20
- **Authors**: Gereon Koßmann And René Schwonnek
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-20-2238/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-20-2238/)
- **Tags**: `efficiency`  `device-independent quantum cryptography`  `conditional von Neumann entropy`  `Navascués-Pironio-Acín hierarchy`  `semidefinite relaxation`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces an efficient numerical framework for estimating conditional von Neumann entropy directly from observed statistics in device-independent quantum cryptography.

### Motivation

Device-independent security and randomness certification require reliable lower bounds on conditional von Neumann entropy from untrusted observed data, but existing methods are computationally demanding.

### Method

The authors use semidefinite relaxations from the Navascués–Pironio–Acín hierarchy combined with a recent integral representation of entropy, reducing the number of support variables and enabling projective variable choices.

### Result

The framework roughly halves the number of support variables compared with existing methods and significantly reduces runtime, making entropy certification practical even under noisy conditions and compatible with entropy accumulation theorems.

### Conclusion

This provides a versatile and efficient tool for device-independent quantum cryptography and randomness generation, broadening secure communication in untrusted environments.

*Keywords: device-independent quantum cryptography, conditional von Neumann entropy, Navascués-Pironio-Acín hierarchy, semidefinite relaxation, entropy accumulation theorem*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces post-selection games, a generalization of nonlocal games with discarded rounds, and develops algorithms for their local and Tsirelson bounds.

### Motivation

Post-selection games formalize possibilistic proofs of nonlocality such as Hardy's paradox and are motivated by Bell tests with low detection efficiency.

### Method

The authors define post-selection games, provide algorithms for computing local and Tsirelson bounds, and analyze their statistical power relative to standard nonlocal games.

### Result

They show that post-selection games can have an unbounded statistical-power advantage over traditional nonlocal games, making them well suited to low-detection-efficiency Bell tests.

### Conclusion

Post-selection games provide a useful theoretical framework for nonlocality with post-selection and inefficient detection, with implications for Bell-test analysis and device-independent reasoning.

*Keywords: nonlocal games, Bell nonlocality, Tsirelson bound, Hardy's paradox, post-selection*

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `entanglement bounds`  `negativity`  `Schmidt number`  `spectrum`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a general framework for upper-bounding bipartite entanglement from a state's spectrum alone, with explicit applications to negativity and Schmidt number.

### Motivation

Entanglement is typically quantified using full state tomography or nonlinear witnesses, but spectral information is often more accessible; the authors ask what rigorous entanglement constraints follow from the spectrum alone.

### Method

Using linear maps and their inverses, they derive analytical sufficient criteria and spectral constraints that bound the maximal entanglement achievable under global unitary transformations for full-rank states in arbitrary dimensions.

### Result

The framework yields compact criteria for bounding negativity and Schmidt number from the spectrum and provides new spectral constraints relevant to Schmidt number witnesses.

### Conclusion

Spectrum-only entanglement bounds offer a practical and general route to certify or constrain entanglement without complete state information, with potential extensions to other entanglement measures.

*Keywords: entanglement bounds, negativity, Schmidt number, spectrum, bipartite quantum states*

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `macroscopic quantum coherence`  `multipartite entanglement`  `quantum macroscopicity`  `mechanical oscillators`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces two quantum-information-theoretic measures of macroscopicity—extensive size and entangled size—and uses them to bound macroscopic coherence and multipartite entanglement in mechanical superposition experiments.

### Motivation

Assessing how close experiments come to macroscopic quantum behavior requires precise, computable, and experimentally measurable notions of quantum macroscopicity, but existing proposals are fragmented and interpretively inconsistent.

### Method

The authors define extensive size via coherence in position or momentum relative to atomic-scale units and entangled size via the number of entangled subsystems, then derive estimation and lower-bound techniques applicable to experimental data from micro-mechanical oscillators and diffracting molecules.

### Result

They obtain lower bounds on macroscopic coherence and find evidence for genuine multipartite entanglement involving a large number of atoms in recent mechanical superposition states.

### Conclusion

The proposed measures unify desirable features of prior macroscopicity proposals while providing rigorous quantum-information interpretations and experimentally accessible lower bounds.

*Keywords: macroscopic quantum coherence, multipartite entanglement, quantum macroscopicity, mechanical oscillators, coherence measures*

- **Tags**: `his`  `entanglement`  `three-qubit entanglement`  `W-entanglement`  `three-tangle`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a polynomial measure of W-entanglement for pure three-qubit states, completing a trio of entanglement measures alongside the three-tangle for GHZ-entanglement and concurrence for bipartite entanglement, and shows these measures and entanglement types are ordered.

### Motivation

Entanglement theory for pure three-qubit states lacked a polynomial measure specifically characterizing W-type entanglement analogous to the three-tangle for GHZ-type entanglement and concurrence for bipartite entanglement.

### Method

The author constructs a polynomial entanglement measure for W-entanglement and compares it with the three-tangle and bipartite concurrence, establishing ordering relations among the measures and the corresponding entanglement classes.

### Result

The three measures are ordered: bipartite concurrence is larger than the W measure, which is larger than the GHZ measure, mirroring an ordering of bipartite, W, and GHZ entanglement types in parallel with the equivalence classes of entangled three-qubit states.

### Conclusion

This work provides an important missing piece in three-qubit entanglement theory and clarifies the hierarchical structure of three-qubit entanglement through polynomial measures.

*Keywords: three-qubit entanglement, W-entanglement, three-tangle, entanglement measures, GHZ entanglement*

## 14. Tripartite Haar Random State Has No Bipartite Entanglement

- **Score**: 8.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. X  |  **Feed**: Physical Review X
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-13
- **Authors**: Zhi Li, Takato Mori, And Beni Yoshida
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/d7b9-ctw2](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/d7b9-ctw2)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Haar random states`  `entanglement distillation`  `bipartite EPR entanglement`  `LOCC`

### TL;DR

Tripartite Haar-random pure states contain no bipartite EPR entanglement that can be distilled by local operations, despite possessing large amounts of bipartite entanglement measures such as entanglement entropy.

### Motivation

Random states are central to quantum information (holography, random tensor networks, Page-curve arguments), and it is important to know whether their bipartite correlations are operationally useful as EPR entanglement under LOCC.

### Method

Analytical arguments using the properties of Haar-random tripartite states, together with entanglement distillation / LOCC monotonicity tools to bound distillable bipartite entanglement across any bipartition.

### Result

It is shown that bipartite EPR entanglement cannot be distilled from tripartite Haar random states by local operations, sharpening the gap between entropic entanglement measures and operationally distillable entanglement in random states.

### Conclusion

Haar-random tripartite states are entanglement-rich by entropic measures yet bipartite-distillation-poor, which has implications for random-state-based models of holography and for resource-theoretic interpretations of random correlations.

*Keywords: Haar random states, entanglement distillation, bipartite EPR entanglement, LOCC, entanglement measures*

## 16. Work Statistics Of Sudden Quantum Quenches: A Random Matrix Theory Perspective On Gaussianity And Its Deviations

- **Score**: 8.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-10-22
- **Authors**: Miguel Tierz
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2241/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2241/)
- **Tags**: `work statistics`  `sudden quench`  `random matrix theory`  `Loschmidt amplitude`  `Gaussianity`

### TL;DR

The paper analyzes work statistics after sudden quantum quenches using random matrix theory, showing Gaussian work distributions for generic quadratic fermionic chains and characterizing non-Gaussian deviations.

### Motivation

Work statistics in non-equilibrium quantum quenches is central to quantum thermodynamics, but universal predictions for its distribution are challenging. The authors aim to identify when the work distribution becomes Gaussian and when non-Gaussian tails arise.

### Method

They map sudden-quench work statistics to traces of powers of Haar-random unitary matrices. For translation-invariant quadratic fermionic chains, the Loschmidt amplitude is expressed via a unitary matrix model/Toeplitz representation, and multivariate central limit theorems for unitary traces are applied.

### Result

They obtain a Gaussian distribution for the work variable with explicit variance and asymptotic independence across powers, and characterize non-Gaussian corrections from many or slowly decaying interactions and Fisher-Hartwig singularities, illustrated in the XY chain with numerical diagnostics.

### Conclusion

Random matrix theory provides a systematic framework for universal work-statistics predictions in sudden quenches and clarifies the mechanisms behind deviations from Gaussianity.

*Keywords: work statistics, sudden quench, random matrix theory, Loschmidt amplitude, Gaussianity*

### Motivation

Long-distance PM-QKD suffers from channel loss that amplifies vacuum emissions and dark counts, degrading parameter estimation and limiting key rate and distance.

The protocol filters local heralding outcomes during key sifting to enhance the single-photon fraction, uses a three-intensity decoy-state method to estimate single-photon contributions and bound the phase-error rate, and provides asymptotic security analysis with simulations.

### TL;DR

The paper shows that a single one-site (minimally entangling/random) gate per layer suffices to generate quantum state designs, revealing a trade-off between dynamical chaos and the amount of randomness needed to form pseudorandom ensembles.

### Motivation

Generating Haar-random states and unitary k-designs normally requires circuits built from highly entangling random two-qubit gates; the authors ask how minimal the randomness in the circuit can be while still producing genuine state designs.

### Method

They analyze ensembles of states produced by brickwork circuits in which only a sparse, one-site set of operations is random, and characterize the resulting ensembles via moments (frame potentials) to test k-design formation, supplemented by diagnostics of dynamical chaos such as operator spreading.

### Result

They find that a single one-site random operation per time step is enough to obtain state designs to high accuracy, and quantify a trade-off in which stronger chaotic dynamics compensates for weaker local randomness.

### Conclusion

Randomness in quantum circuits can be drastically economized without losing design properties, giving insight into the interplay between chaos, scrambling, and pseudorandomness and offering practical implications for randomized benchmarking and shadow tomography protocols.

*Keywords: quantum state designs, random quantum circuits, dynamical chaos, pseudorandomness, k-designs*

## 109. A Counterexample To The Optimality Conjecture In Convex Quantum Channel Optimization

- **Score**: 6.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-28
- **Authors**: Jianting Yang
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-28-2253/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-28-2253/)
- **Tags**: `his`  `quantum channel optimization`  `convex optimization`  `trace distance`  `dual certificate`

### TL;DR

A 2-dimensional counterexample disproves the conjecture that applying the sign function to the error matrix uniquely yields a dual certificate for every optimal solution of the trace-distance-based optimal state transformation problem.

### Motivation

Coutts et al. (Quantum 5, 448, 2021) proposed a convex-optimization formulation of optimal state transformation under the trace distance and conjectured a universal dual-certificate construction via the sign function; verifying or refuting such structural claims is essential for trusting the resulting numerical and analytical tools.

### Method

The author constructs an explicit family of quantum channels/states on 2-dimensional Hilbert spaces where the optimal solution's error matrix yields a sign-function-based dual candidate that fails to be a valid certificate, thereby violating uniqueness.

### Result

The constructed example satisfies the problem's optimality conditions yet admits a sign-function dual candidate that is not a dual certificate, so the conjecture fails already in the smallest Hilbert-space dimension.

### Conclusion

The optimality conjecture of Coutts et al. is false in general; dual certificates for trace-distance-based channel optimization must be derived by other means, and the scope of validity of the original conjecture is narrowed.

*Keywords: quantum channel optimization, convex optimization, trace distance, dual certificate, state transformation*

- **Tags**: `his`  `optomechanics`  `photonic-crystal cavity`  `flexural modes`  `nanomechanical interconnects`

### TL;DR

Demonstrates lithographically engineered optomechanical nanobeam cavities with serpentine mechanical interconnects enabling optical readout and tunable coupling of MHz flexural modes.

### Motivation

Integrated nanomechanical circuits need compact, predictable ways to read out, confine, and connect mechanical motion across many nanoscale elements, especially for extended MHz flexural modes in dense optomechanical devices.

### Method

The authors break dispersive-coupling cancellation using transverse geometric asymmetry in a photonic-crystal cavity, making in-plane flexural modes optically bright, and design serpentine mechanical links as compact mechanical mirrors and evanescent couplers; they measure coupled-cavity normal-mode splitting versus number of serpentine cells and compare with full-system simulations.

### Result

The normal-mode splitting decays exponentially with the number of serpentine cells, yielding an attenuation constant in quantitative agreement with simulations; coupling is tunable by interconnect design, and finite-link modes can hybridize with cavity modes beyond a simple two-resonator picture.

### Conclusion

Complex-band-engineered mechanical links provide calibrated interconnects for scalable optomechanical nanocircuits based on optically addressable MHz flexural resonators.

## 129. Failure Of The Mean-field Hartree Approximation For A Bosonic Many-body System With non-Hermitian Hamiltonian

- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2244/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2244/)
- **Tags**: `his`  `spectrum`  `non-Hermitian Hamiltonian`  `mean-field Hartree approximation`  `bosonic many-body systems`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that the mean-field Hartree approximation fails for general non-Hermitian bosonic many-body Hamiltonians, with explicit counterexamples and a finite-time transition to a mixed one-particle marginal state.

### Motivation

Non-Hermitian Hamiltonians are used to model particle gain/loss and open-system dynamics between quantum jumps, and the Hartree approximation is central to reducing many-body dynamics to effective one-particle evolution, including in proposed quantum algorithms.

### Method

The authors prove that for general two-body bosonic mean-field Hamiltonians the non-Hermitian Hartree equation fails to capture the correct second time derivative of the one-particle marginal at early times; they also analytically solve an anti-Hermitian bosonic-qubit model and simulate a non-Hermitian Hamiltonian with real spectrum.

### Result

The exact thermodynamic-limit one-particle marginal disagrees with the non-Hermitian Hartree solution, and for certain initial conditions the exact marginal undergoes a finite-time transition to a mixed state, a phenomenon absent for Hermitian Hamiltonians.

### Conclusion

The mean-field Hartree approximation is not generally valid for non-Hermitian bosonic many-body systems; additional conditions are needed to justify its use for modeling gain/loss and open-system dynamics.

*Keywords: non-Hermitian Hamiltonian, mean-field Hartree approximation, bosonic many-body systems, open quantum systems, one-particle marginal*

### TL;DR

QwaveMPS is an open-source Python package that uses matrix product states to efficiently simulate non-Markovian, nonlinear waveguide-QED dynamics with quantized atoms and photons treated on equal footing.

### Motivation

Full Hilbert-space simulations of one-dimensional waveguide-QED systems with time-delayed feedback and quantized photons are computationally prohibitive, so a scalable tensor-network tool is needed to make such studies practical.

### Method

The package automates waveguide-QED-specific tensor-network operations: building the time-bin evolution operator from physical parameters, constructing the swap network for delayed interactions, managing orthogonality centers and truncation, factorizing multi-photon Fock-state inputs as MPS, and evaluating single- and two-time field observables such as g(2).

### Result

QwaveMPS enables efficient simulation of complex dynamical interactions—including non-Markovian time-delayed feedback and deeply nonlinear regimes—at greatly reduced computational cost compared with full Hilbert-space approaches, in both Markovian and non-Markovian regimes.

### Conclusion

The tool provides a user-friendly, scalable platform for modeling open waveguide-QED systems, lowering the barrier to studying atom-photon dynamics relevant to quantum information and photonic quantum hardware.

*Keywords: waveguide QED, matrix product states, non-Markovian dynamics, open quantum systems, photon correlation functions*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a quantum analogue of classical synchronizing automata and shows how a unitary resetting protocol can drive a qudit automaton to a fixed pure state while encoding the initial configuration in auxiliary qubits.

### Conclusion

The work provides a unitary realization of synchronizing automata and a mechanism for generating specific entangled states. It connects automata theory with quantum information concepts such as resetting and entanglement, though it remains a specialized theoretical contribution.

### TL;DR

A Floquet-based framework derives commutator-scaling error bounds for time-dependent product and multiproduct Trotter formulas, tightening gate-count estimates for simulating driven dynamics and adiabatic state preparation.

### Motivation

Product formulas (Trotter-Suzuki decompositions) are the workhorse of digital quantum simulation, but rigorous error bounds for time-dependent Hamiltonians and multiproduct extrapolation schemes have been loose, leading to overly pessimistic resource estimates.

### Method

The authors recast the stroboscopic dynamics of product formulas as an effective Floquet Hamiltonian and bound the error in terms of nested commutators of the Hamiltonian and its time derivatives, extending the analysis to multiproduct (Richardson-type) formulas.

### Result

The derived bounds scale more favorably with the number of Trotter steps and reproduce the expected order-by-order error scaling, yielding sharper gate-count estimates for driven-dynamics simulation and adiabatic state preparation.

### Conclusion

The Floquet-based commutator bounds provide a practical and rigorous tool for resource estimation in time-dependent Hamiltonian simulation, though the contribution is primarily algorithmic rather than information-theoretic.

### Motivation

Gaussian boson sampling is a leading route to demonstrating quantum computational advantage, but its hardness argument relies on the unproven hiding conjecture, which concerns approximating submatrices of random orthogonal matrices by complex symmetric Gaussian matrices.

### Method

The authors analyze submatrices of Haar-random unitary symmetric matrices and show that an M×M submatrix of an N×N circular orthogonal ensemble matrix is close in total variation distance to a complex symmetric Gaussian matrix as N grows.

### Result

They prove the hiding conjecture for GBS with all input modes squeezed, the first rigorous proof in the experimentally relevant regime, making the hardness argument comparable to that of conventional boson sampling.

### Conclusion

### Method

The authors introduce site-centered defect tensors that can be created, moved, and fused by local unitaries, then define a block-independence condition under which symmetry defects can be promoted to gauge degrees of freedom.

The paper extends quantum criticality to dynamically stable quadratic bosonic Hamiltonians that may lack a thermodynamic ground state, using a quasiparticle vacuum and a Krein spectral gap.

### Motivation

Standard notions of quantum criticality rely on thermodynamic stability and ground states, but physically relevant quadratic bosonic models can be thermodynamically unstable, motivating a broader dynamical-systems-based framework.

### Method

The authors define a Gaussian quasiparticle vacuum for dynamically stable QBHs, introduce the Krein gap as the relevant spectral gap, and analyze correlations, entanglement entropy, and information-geometric indicators near exceptional points and Krein collisions.

### Result

They prove exponential decay of correlations when the Krein gap is positive, while gap closure via exceptional points or Krein collisions can produce long-range correlations and critical or multicritical behavior even without thermodynamic stability.

### Conclusion

The Krein gap plays the role of the spectral gap for dynamically stable QBHs, unifying dynamical stability boundaries with criticality and enabling critical-phenomena studies in photonic, optomechanical, cavity-QED, and magnonic platforms.

The paper proposes a quantum-data re-uploading architecture that uses sequential interactions with fresh copies of an input quantum state to achieve universal approximation of bounded continuous functions.

### Motivation

Quantum data re-uploading is well established for classical inputs, but extending it to quantum inputs is underexplored because quantum-state information cannot be directly accessed classically.

### Method

A qubit interacts sequentially with fresh copies of an arbitrary input state, with entangling unitaries alternating with mid-circuit resets to realize a discrete cascade of completely positive and trace-preserving maps analogous to collision models.

### Result

The architecture can approximate any bounded continuous function using one ancilla qubit and single-qubit measurements, given sufficiently many input copies and, in general, sufficiently many layers.

### Conclusion

This provides a space-efficient and expressive framework for quantum machine learning directly on quantum data, though its relevance to core quantum information theory is limited.

*Keywords: quantum data re-uploading, universal function approximation, quantum machine learning, CPTP maps, collision models*

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Spin-charge separation in Tomonaga-Luttinger liquids is well established in terms of velocity differences, but probing the anomalous dimensions of emergent excitations remains experimentally challenging.

### Method

A dissipative response theory scheme that selectively dissipates spin-1/2 particles and measures the resulting spin response is analyzed numerically using time-dependent density-matrix renormalization group, with microscopic parameters matched to Luttinger parameters via Bethe ansatz.

### Result

The response shows a universal short-time power-law growth crossing over to linear-in-time growth, with a crossover time scaling proportional to a parameter whose slope encodes interaction-dependent Luttinger exponents; the spatial decay of the short-time law yields the sum of anomalous dimensions, and the signal vanishes in the noninteracting limit.

### Conclusion

Off-diagonal dissipative response is established as a self-contained, calibration-free probe of quantum fractionalization in synthetic quantum matter.

- **Tags**: `his`  `disorder`  `renormalization-group`  `tricritical Ising CFT`  `symmetry-enriched topological phase`

### TL;DR

The authors construct a symmetry-enriched 'topological' tricritical Ising critical point in a cluster O'Brien–Fendley spin chain and show that, despite sharing bulk CFT and conventional boundary CFT data with the original model, it exhibits a spontaneously fixed boundary condition distinguished by time-reversal charges.

### Motivation

Quantum critical points can host symmetry-protected topological edge modes even with a gapless bulk, raising the question of whether paradigmatic CFTs such as the supersymmetric tricritical Ising point admit topologically nontrivial, symmetry-enriched realizations with distinct boundary physics.

### Method

An SPT entangler is applied to the original O'Brien–Fendley chain to build the cluster model sharing the same bulk TCI CFT; boundary conditions, boundary RG flows, and finite-size energy splittings under symmetry-preserving boundary perturbations are analyzed analytically and numerically, including a two-parameter phase diagram.

### Result

The cluster realization shows a spontaneously fixed boundary condition (spontaneous boundary magnetization) with exponential finite-size splitting, in contrast to the algebraic splitting of the symmetry-enriched Ising point, and cannot be converted to the free boundary condition by the studied deformations; the topological TCI persists along a critical line.

### Conclusion

A symmetry-enriched TCI universality class is established, revealing boundary distinctions invisible to conventional boundary CFT data such as boundary operator content and g-function.

*Keywords: tricritical Ising CFT, symmetry-enriched topological phase, boundary conformal field theory, symmetry-protected topological entangler, boundary renormalization group flow*

### Motivation

Existing quantum heuristics for combinatorial optimization, such as QAOA and variational guided quantum walks, rely on costly classical outer-loop optimization that becomes impractical beyond the NISQ regime; the authors seek an optimizer-free alternative.

### Result

Applied to MaxCut, maximum independent set, portfolio optimization, LABS, MAX-k-SAT and a quartic TSP reformulation, the algorithm finds high-quality approximate solutions for instances up to dozens of qubits while sampling only polynomially many states, comparing favorably with classically optimized variational approaches and deep-circuit QAOA.

### Conclusion

### Motivation

Biological immune-like robustness is typically studied in complex living systems; the author asks whether an analogous protective property can be formalized and quantified in well-mixed, deterministic open chemical reaction networks far from equilibrium.

### Method

Open chemical reaction networks with fixed chemostats are modeled under deterministic mass-action-type dynamics, and proto-immunity is defined via constraints on sustained nonequilibrium fluxes/throughput, with conditions derived for when the network can preserve a minimal throughput despite changes in the environment or network parameters.

### Result

The framework yields criteria characterizing when a network can buffer perturbations and maintain a prescribed minimum dissipative throughput, relating immune-like protection to the underlying stoichiometric and thermodynamic structure of the network.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

The paper generalizes the standard Lévy walk model to include permanent particle immobilization arising from a non-normalized (improper) flight-time distribution, and derives the resulting transport statistics.

### Motivation

Permanent immobilization is ubiquitous in complex transport systems, from intracellular transport to active matter in disordered media, but existing Lévy walk models assume normalizable flight-time distributions and cannot capture particles that stop indefinitely.

### Method

Starting from the standard Lévy walk with power-law flight times, the authors modify the flight-time distribution so that it is non-normalized, assigning finite weight to infinite trapping times, and analyze the resulting propagator and moments via continuous-time random walk / fractional-diffusion style techniques.

### Result

The modified model yields a fraction of permanently immobilized particles coexisting with a mobile population, altering the asymptotic spreading laws, the effective diffusion exponent, and the shape of the position distribution relative to conventional Lévy walks.

### Conclusion

Non-normalized flight-time statistics provide a natural, analytically tractable route to describing transport with permanent trapping, with implications for anomalous transport in disordered and biological media.

*Keywords: Lévy walk, anomalous transport, immobilization, flight-time distribution, continuous-time random walk*

### Motivation

A long-standing claim held that dimensional reduction breaks supersymmetry at three loops in SYM theories, since prior computations found disagreeing Yukawa and ghost-gluon anomalous dimensions; resolving this is important for validating DR as a consistent supersymmetric regulator.

### Method

### Result

The previously reported mismatch is resolved: the Yukawa and ghost-gluon functions are shown to agree at three loops, so DR preserves supersymmetry at this order.

### Conclusion

### Result

Closed-form and unified expressions for the correlation functions are obtained, revealing the interrelations and structural analogies among Bessel, Mathieu, parabolic and plane-wave families.

### Conclusion

The framework provides a common language for describing coherence of nondiffracting beams, with relevance to classical structured-light optics rather than to quantum information, thermodynamics, networks, or hybrid quantum platforms.

### Result

The interfaces sustain robustly high thermal conductance that remains essentially unchanged under sliding and rotation, indicating strong insensitivity to contact geometry and motion.

- **Tags**: `his`  `field shift`  `King plot`  `atomic spectroscopy`  `nuclear charge radius`

### TL;DR

The paper systematically expands field-shift energies in atomic spectra to higher orders and shows that, under a configuration-independent ratio approximation, these corrections do not induce King-plot nonlinearities.

### Motivation

Higher-order field-shift contributions affect King-plot linearity used in searches for new physics, and their understanding is needed to interpret observed nonlinearities.

### Method

The authors derive a systematic expansion of field-shift energies in nuclear parameters and validate it against direct numerical calculations for H-like ions; they also compute corrections for alkali-like systems from Li-like to Rb-like ions.

### Result

Ratios of higher-order to leading field-shift corrections are nearly independent of ionic charge state and agree with hydrogenic ratios at sub-percent level; within the proposed approximation, higher-order corrections do not cause King-plot nonlinearities.

### Conclusion

Only small contributions beyond the configuration-independent ratio approximation can explain experimentally observed King-plot nonlinearities, constraining new-physics searches.

*Keywords: field shift, King plot, atomic spectroscopy, nuclear charge radius, higher-order corrections*

### TL;DR

The paper shows that critical self-exciting Hawkes point processes with random fertilities can remain stationary in law even when mean activity diverges, challenging the standard assumption that exact criticality precludes stationarity.

### Motivation

Critical dynamics are invoked to describe many natural and social systems, but exact criticality is conventionally believed to be incompatible with stationarity because the mean activity diverges; the author questions whether this belief is generally valid.

### Method

Analytical treatment of Hawkes point processes with random fertility (branching) distributions at criticality, deriving conditions under which stationarity in law holds despite infinite mean activity.

### Result

Criticality does not in general preclude stationarity for self-exciting Hawkes processes; stationarity in law can persist beyond the infinite-mean-activity regime when fertilities are random.

### Conclusion

The standard link between criticality and non-stationarity is not universal, broadening the class of stationary critical point-process models applicable to social and natural systems.

*Keywords: Hawkes process, criticality, stationarity in law, random fertility, point processes*

### Motivation

Tidal resonances excited during binary neutron star inspiral encode information about the internal structure and equation of state of neutron stars, but their detectability with current gravitational-wave detectors remains undetermined.

### Method

The authors model the resonant excitation of neutron-star vibrational modes by the rising tidal frequency during inspiral and assess the resulting imprint on the gravitational-wave phasing relative to detector sensitivity.

### Result

The analysis establishes the conditions and parameter regimes under which tidal resonance effects could leave a detectable signature in the gravitational-wave signal.

### Conclusion

Detecting tidal resonances would provide a novel seismological probe of neutron-star interiors, though detectability depends strongly on mode properties and detector sensitivity.

### TL;DR

Discrete-element simulations of a vibrated two-dimensional hopper show that granular clogging involves structural consolidation and mechanically stable arch formation beyond the memoryless Poisson picture.

### Motivation

Clogging at narrow constrictions is usually modeled as a memoryless Poisson process, which captures when clogs start but not how stable arches emerge and persist.

### Method

The authors run discrete-element simulations of a vibrated 2D hopper undergoing repeated clogging events and analyze the evolving force network and grain geometry.

### Result

They identify a structural consolidation process during arch formation, distinguishing transient from persistent clogs and revealing memory effects absent in the Poisson description.

### Conclusion

Granular clogging is not purely Poissonian; the mechanics of arch consolidation must be included to predict clog stability, with implications for hopper and flow design.

*Keywords: granular clogging, arch formation, discrete-element method, Poisson process, hopper flow*

### Motivation

Cantor's continuum problem—determining the size of the reals—remains a central open question, and since |R|=|N^2| it can be recast as a classification problem for cardinal exponentiation that Gödel and Cohen showed is independent of ZFC.

Set-theoretic analysis of cardinal exponentiation under Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory with Choice, proposing and investigating an 'ABCD hypothesis' as a classification principle.

### Result

The paper formulates the ABCD hypothesis and explores its consequences for the structure of cardinal exponentiation and the continuum problem.

### Conclusion

The work contributes to foundational set theory and the metamathematics of the continuum, with no connection to quantum information, thermodynamics, networks, or hybrid quantum systems.

### TL;DR

The paper anchors Anatolian tree-ring records and Mesopotamian chronology using single-year radiocarbon patterns combined with ice-core evidence to resolve long-standing chronological debates.

### Motivation

Competing chronological schemes for ancient Mesopotamia have persisted for decades, hampering the synchronization of civilizations across the Near East and eastern Mediterranean.

### Method

Single-year radiocarbon measurements from Anatolian tree-ring sequences are matched as patterns against ice-core records and compared with candidate absolute chronologies.

### Result

The single-year radiocarbon patterns provide a constraint that discriminates among competing chronological schemes and supports an anchored absolute timeline.

### Conclusion

High-resolution radiocarbon pattern matching, cross-validated with ice-core evidence, offers a route to resolving Mesopotamian chronological uncertainty.

Restoration of effective adaptive immunity against chronic viral infection after immune exhaustion is rare, and the postpartum spontaneous suppression of HCV provides a natural model to study viral escape and immune control.

### TL;DR

A paleoclimate study using multiproxy records and climate modeling to explain Holocene millennial-scale climate oscillations through tropical–Antarctic ocean–atmosphere interactions.

### Motivation

Since Bond's discovery of millennial-scale Holocene climate events in North Atlantic ice-rafted debris records, the mechanism driving these oscillations has remained unexplained.

### Method

Multiproxy paleoclimate reconstructions combined with climate model simulations (including PMIP-style coupled model experiments) are used to diagnose tropical–Antarctic ocean–atmosphere coupling.

### Result

The analysis attributes Holocene millennial-scale climate variability to interactions between tropical and Antarctic oceanic and atmospheric processes rather than to a single North Atlantic driver.

### Conclusion

Millennial climate oscillations in the Holocene are best explained by interhemispheric ocean–atmosphere interactions linking the tropics and Antarctica, offering a unifying mechanism for the Bond events.

*Keywords: Holocene climate variability, millennial-scale oscillations, Bond events, paleoclimate modeling, tropical–Antarctic teleconnections*

### Motivation

Human exploration of Mars requires thorough characterization of surface hazards, particularly respirable dust, before crews arrive; existing dust assessments have not resolved the fine and ultrafine particle fractions in detail.

### Method

In situ X-ray fluorescence measurements from the Perseverance rover are analyzed to determine the composition and grain-size distribution of fine (≲2.5 µm) and ultrafine (≲0.1 µm) Martian dust, which are then evaluated against known toxicity and mechanical-hazard criteria.

### Result

The data reveal that the fine and ultrafine dust fractions carry compositions and particle sizes that present additional risks beyond those previously catalogued for coarser regolith dust.

Mission planners should account for these fine and ultrafine dust hazards in crewed Mars architectures, including filtration, dust mitigation, and toxicological exposure limits.

The authors combine genetic manipulation of miR-219 and RalGPS with neural stimulation paradigms and electrophysiological/imaging assays at the Drosophila neuromuscular junction.

### Result

Neural stimulation selectively alters miR-219 levels, and disrupting miR-219–RalGPS regulation impairs structural and functional synaptic plasticity.

### Conclusion

An activity-regulated miRNA–target module is essential for synaptic plasticity, implicating post-transcriptional control in neuronal adaptation.

*Keywords: microRNA, miR-219, RalGPS, synaptic plasticity, Drosophila neuromuscular junction*

Myoblast fusion is essential for embryonic muscle development and for regeneration after injury or disease, but the molecular machinery beyond Myomaker that mediates membrane fusion remains incompletely understood.

### TL;DR

This study examines how calcium-permeability-enhancing mutations in the GluRIIC subunit suppress sustained presynaptic homeostatic plasticity caused by loss of GluRIIA at the Drosophila neuromuscular junction.

### Motivation

Neurons homeostatically adjust synaptic strength to keep circuits stable, but the molecular mechanisms that sustain this plasticity over time remain poorly understood; the authors ask whether calcium influx through AMPA-type glutamate receptors is required to maintain it.

### Method

Using Drosophila genetics, the authors combine GluRIIA loss-of-function with calcium-permeability-enhancing mutations in GluRIIC and assess synaptic physiology and homeostatic plasticity at the neuromuscular junction.

### Result

The calcium-permeability-enhancing GluRIIC mutations suppress the sustained presynaptic homeostatic plasticity that normally follows loss of GluRIIA, implicating altered receptor calcium flux in the maintenance of synaptic homeostasis.

### Conclusion

Calcium permeability of glutamate receptors critically regulates whether presynaptic homeostatic plasticity can be sustained, offering molecular insight into circuit stability mechanisms.

The authors present a fully human organ-on-a-chip model of head and neck squamous cell carcinoma that incorporates engineered extracellular matrix, vascularization, and innervation to study tumor behavior.

Understanding solid tumors requires in vitro models that capture the combined influence of extracellular matrix, vasculature, and innervation on tumor progression, which conventional culture systems fail to reproduce.

The team built a microfluidic HNSCC-on-a-chip platform on defined and tunable engineered extracellular matrices, integrating vascular and neuronal components to mimic the tumor microenvironment.

The model supports innervated and vascularized tumor growth, enabling controlled study of matrix and stromal contributions to HNSCC behavior.

- **Tags**: `his`  `gasdermin D`  `SMYD3 methyltransferase`  `pyroptosis`  `lysine dimethylation`

### TL;DR

The study identifies SMYD3 as a methyltransferase that dimethylates gasdermin D at lysine 43, thereby promoting pyroptosis and inflammatory responses.

### Motivation

GSDMD activation must be tightly controlled to trigger pyroptosis while avoiding excessive inflammation, yet the post-translational modifications governing this balance remain incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors combine biochemical methyltransferase assays, site-specific mutagenesis of GSDMD lysine residues, and cellular/in vivo inflammatory models to characterize SMYD3-dependent dimethylation.

### Result

SMYD3 directly catalyzes GSDMD dimethylation at K43, which enhances GSDMD pore-forming activity, pyroptosis, and downstream inflammatory cytokine release.

### Conclusion

SMYD3-mediated K43 dimethylation of GSDMD constitutes a regulatory layer controlling pyroptosis and inflammation, suggesting a potential target for inflammatory disease intervention.

Goodmanstefan Wagerjure Leskovecahttps://ror.org/00f54p054department Of Computer Science, Stanford University, Stanford, CA 94305bhttps://ror.org/01an7q238department Of Computer Science, University Of California, Berkeley, CA 94720chttps://ror.org/05a28rw58department Of Biosystems Science And Engineering, ETH Zurich, Basel 4056, Switzerlanddhttps://ror.org/00f54p054department Of Epidemiology And Population Health, Stanford University, Stanford, CA 94305ehttps://ror.org/00f54p054stanford Graduate School Of Business, Stanford University, Stanford, CA 94305

- **Tags**: `Long COVID`  `claims data`  `epidemiology`  `symptom duration`  `misattribution bias`

### TL;DR

This study uses insurance claims data from 245 million US patients to revise estimates of the types and duration of Long COVID symptoms, correcting for misattribution biases in commonly used study designs.

### Motivation

Many existing Long COVID studies misattribute symptoms to COVID-19 when they are actually caused by non-COVID conditions that prompted care-seeking and testing, leading to inflated or distorted estimates of symptom prevalence and persistence.

### Method

The authors analyze large-scale medical claims records covering 245 million US patients, applying statistical/epidemiological designs (likely including negative-control or matched-cohort comparisons) to separate genuine post-COVID sequelae from background care-seeking effects.

### Result

The analysis yields revised estimates of the types and duration of Long COVID symptoms that differ from earlier claims-based figures, indicating that some prior estimates overstate the burden attributable to COVID-19.

### Conclusion

Careful study design is essential when using claims data to quantify Long COVID; the revised estimates provide a more accurate picture of symptom types and persistence at national scale.

*Keywords: Long COVID, claims data, epidemiology, symptom duration, misattribution bias*
